**Top remaining risks**

1. 2 parameter(s) BLOCKED — no evaluated value meets the policy: baseRatioBps[1], baseRatioBps[2].
2. At the recommended set the G3 study (worst ensemble member) puts P(bad debt at claim opening) above the policy for class B 2.74% vs 1.0%, C 20.44% vs 2.0% — no liquidation before claimHeight (fact 1.5-1).
3. Every recommendation rests on synthetic data (PROVISIONAL); lock-readiness needs a year of real hourly YEC/USD, a spreads.py log and ideally pool-share data.

Verdicts: **KEEP** — the current value stays; no candidate improved the primary metric by more than the policy's materiality without violating a constraint; **CHANGE** — move to the recommended value; it satisfies every policy constraint and improves the primary metric materially (or the current value violates the policy); **PROVISIONAL** — the study's answer rests on synthetic data only; treat it as a direction, not a value to lock; **BLOCKED** — no evaluated value satisfies the policy; the owner must relax the policy or the rule; **NOT RUN** — the group's study is not available in this build or raised an error; no recommendation.
Non-tunable parameters (derived, per-release, constants, identity) are listed in Appendix A.

## 2. Policy and data provenance

Policy: `/Users/boy/projects/yellowback-workspace/wt/ybcal-audit/policy/default.toml` (sha256 of the parsed policy `24a0b7ebfacf1b67…`), used verbatim:

```toml
# ybcal risk policy — the owner's risk appetite (PLAN §2.4, §5, §12).
#
# Copy this file, edit it, and pass it with `--policy FILE`. Every value is a STARTING TOLERANCE for
# the owner to confirm, not a decision. Section headers only group keys: every key below is a field
# of `ybcal.config.Policy`, and keys are unique across sections. A table whose name is itself a
# Policy field (e.g. [risk.max_bad_debt_prob]) is that field's value.
#
# Units are in the comment on each line. Probabilities are fractions (0.01 = 1 %). Shares are
# fractions of hashrate or attestor weight. Money in policy is in USD (floats are fine here: the
# policy is a tolerance, never a consensus quantity).

[general]
materiality = 0.20                 # min relative improvement of a param's primary metric to move off its current value (§2.3, §12.2)
seed = 20261003                    # default RNG seed for every run (overridden by --seed)
require_real_data_for_lock = true  # lock-readiness needs real-data provenance for every locked param (§7)

[risk]
class_heterogeneity_max = 0.50     # max relative spread of P(bad debt) within one class before a split/merge is suggested (§5.3)
term_distribution = "uniform"      # lock-term distribution within each class: "uniform" | "short-heavy" | "long-heavy" (§5.3)
sigma_mult_at = "median"           # σ multiplier assumed when sizing base ratios: "median" | "p90" (§5.3)
price_drift = "centred"            # drift of long-horizon solvency price paths: "centred" (median flat) | "martingale" (E[p] flat, −σ²/2 bleed) | "model" (D-RD-AUD-1)

[risk.max_bad_debt_prob]           # max P(collateral < debt when the claim path opens), per term class (§5.3, §12.1)
A = 0.005
B = 0.01
C = 0.02

[claims]
claimant_min_profit_bps = 200      # bps of vault value a claimant must net after fees to show up (§5.3)
claimant_slippage_pctl = 90        # percentile of liquidation slippage used for claimant profit (§5.3)

[oracle]
attack_share_min = 0.34            # smallest colluding hash share that may be able to move a median (§5.1, §12.1)
max_no_price_hours = 6.0           # max expected hours/year with NO_PRICE set (§5.1)
attack_moved_tol = 0.05            # max fraction of attack blocks in which a coalition at attack_share_min moves the harmful price (G1, D-WP7a-2)
pool_outage_rate_per_day = 0.033333333333333  # per-pool feed outages per day assumed in G1/G2 availability (D-WP7a-3)

pump_overpricing_lambda = 0.5      # weight of pump overpricing vs crash-lag CVaR in the G1 objective (§5.1)
crash_lag_cvar_alpha = 0.95        # CVaR level for crash tracking lag (§5.1)

sigma_accept_band = [1.0, 1.5]     # M14 acceptance band for the median multiplier at realised volatility (§5.2)
hour_kernel_tolerance_bps = 300.0  # max p95 relative error (bps) of the hour-mode oracle kernel vs block mode (§3.3)

[activation]
max_false_halt_hours_per_year = 24.0  # max expected hours/year of false PARTICIPATION/ENFORCEMENT halts (§5.5, §12.1)

[abandonment]

runbook_operator_buffer_blocks = 4608  # operator buffer in the freeze-then-fix runbook (§5.4), 4 days

release_tip_date = "2026-10-02"    # date of release_tip
# Optional, unset by default (TOML has no null; add the key to set it):
# yec_daily_volume_p10_usd = 150000.0   # p10 daily YEC trading volume, USD — enables the maxMint/supply-cap depth checks
# reference_price_usd = 0.40            # YEC/USD reference price for fee/bond judgements (default: from data)
# next_upgrade_height = 3500000         # next scheduled Ycash network upgrade (default: read from chainparams; none scheduled)

[studies_g6_g7]  # assumptions of the G6/G7 studies (D-WP7d-6)
liar_bias_bps = 2000                 # quote bias of the liar the judgement rules must catch, bps (G6)
min_liar_detection = 0.9                  # min probability REG-4 penalises that liar (G6)
max_fee0_prob = 0.001                # max probability a block has no eligible payee (FEE-0) (G6)
min_registered_prob = 0.999                # min probability an honest pool is registered (REG-1) (G6)
min_liar_exclusion = 0.99                 # min probability the wallet payee rule excludes a liar (G6)
max_honest_exclusion = 0.01                 # max probability the wallet payee rule excludes an honest pool (G6)
max_accuracy_sd_bps = 500                  # max sd of honest pools' accuracy score, bps (G6)
max_honest_payee_spread = 1.5                  # max ratio between honest pools' payee weights (G6)
min_accuracy_premium = 0.25                 # min payee-weight premium of an accurate over a sloppy pool (G6)
p10_daily_volume_usd = 25000.0              # PLACEHOLDER p10 daily YEC volume, USD, used by G7 only when no depth file and no yec_daily_volume_p10_usd (G7)
system_ratio_alarm_bps = 15000                # HALT-2 must fire before the true system ratio reaches this, bps (G7)

```

#### <a id="p-pFastWindow"></a>§3.1 `pFastWindow` — PROVISIONAL

**96 blocks (2 h) → 96 blocks (2 h)** · locked / locked · confidence low · provenance synthetic
- **What it controls:** Fast price-median window. Read by: PRICE-1, PRICE-2, HALT-3, SIGMA-1, MINT-10.
- **Decision rule:** Among window sets whose every median needs a colluding hash share ≥ attack_share_min (34%) to control it (V16, exact binomial) and in which a 34% coalition moves pMint up or pClaim down by half its bias in ≤ 5 % of attack blocks (simulated), with background NO_PRICE ≤ max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr) and HALT-3 still firing within a day on ≥ halt_recall_floor (90%) of 70 % one-day crashes: minimise J = CVaR95%(pClaim 90 % crash lag)/current + λ·E[pMint − true | pump-dump]/current (λ = 0.5). Keep the current windows unless J improves by more than materiality (20%); ties go to the current values. Min-fills follow (L9).
- **Binding constraint:** better-scoring sets violate max_no_price_hours (15 sets); at the search bound of pMidWindow
- **Sensitivity:** the G1 objective is moderate in pFastWindow across 1–4 hours: it rises as pFastWindow increases. Joint sensitivity at ±1 step: ST of factor 'G1 group' (grouped) — system P(bad debt) 0.44, minting halted by price (h/yr) 0.01, false activation halts (h/yr) 0.00, oracle attack share 0.00; it moves system P(bad debt) most.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (48 blocks (1 h)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value); +1 step (144 blocks (3 h)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

pFastWindow sets the fast price median (pFast): the lower median of the last pFastWindow blocks' quote tags. pMint is the minimum of the three medians, so pFast is what lets the minting price fall quickly in a crash; HALT-3 compares it with pMid; SIGMA-1 measures volatility on it; MINT-10 compares attested prices with it (rules: PRICE-1, PRICE-2, HALT-3, SIGMA-1, MINT-10). Current 96 blocks (2 h); recommended 96 blocks (2 h) — verdict PROVISIONAL (provenance synthetic, confidence low).

Decision rule: Among window sets whose every median needs a colluding hash share ≥ attack_share_min (34%) to control it (V16, exact binomial) and in which a 34% coalition moves pMint up or pClaim down by half its bias in ≤ 5 % of attack blocks (simulated), with background NO_PRICE ≤ max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr) and HALT-3 still firing within a day on ≥ halt_recall_floor (90%) of 70 % one-day crashes: minimise J = CVaR95%(pClaim 90 % crash lag)/current + λ·E[pMint − true | pump-dump]/current (λ = 0.5). Keep the current windows unless J improves by more than materiality (20%); ties go to the current values. Min-fills follow (L9).

At the current windows the crash-lag CVaR is 46.5 h, pump overpricing 171 bps, the smallest controlling coalition 39.7% of hash, background NO_PRICE 20.5 h/yr (16.5 h per 6-hour feed outage) and HALT-3 recall on a one-day 70 % crash 100%.

Binding: better-scoring sets violate max_no_price_hours (15 sets); at the search bound of pMidWindow.

the G1 objective is moderate in pFastWindow across 1–4 hours: it rises as pFastWindow increases.

Neighbours: 48 scores +0.0% on the crash lag; 144 scores +0.0% on the crash lag.

Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19). PROVISIONAL: only synthetic prices back this; rerun with real YEC data (`ybcal data fetch`) before locking.

#### <a id="p-sigmaRefBps"></a>§3.6 `sigmaRefBps` — PROVISIONAL

**10,000 bps (100 %) → 7,000 bps (70 %)** · locked / locked · confidence low · provenance synthetic
- **What it controls:** Reference annualised volatility; multiplier = σ/σref clamped; 0 = multiplier fixed at 1. Read by: SIGMA-1, M14.
- **Decision rule:** sigmaRefBps = the median σ̂ of the realised-volatility ensemble, measured on simulated pFast (D-WP3-6) at the recommended volWindow/volStep, rounded down to a multiple of 500 bps (so the unclamped median multiplier is ≥ 1×). #### <a id="p-claimThresholdBps"></a>§3.15 `claimThresholdBps` — PROVISIONAL

**11,000 bps (110 %) → 11,000 bps (110 %)** · locked / locked · confidence low · provenance synthetic · *insensitive*
- **What it controls:** Collateral ratio below which a matured vault is claimable. Read by: RED-4, RED-5.
- **Decision rule:** claimThresholdBps = the smallest threshold (250-bps lattice) at which the expected claimant margin at the first RED-4(a) trigger — collateral less FEE-1 and the network fee, sold at the true price less the policy slippage, minus the YED burned — is ≥ claimant_min_profit_bps (worst ensemble member); KEEP unless more than materiality lower; BLOCKED (highest margin) when no threshold within bounds clears it.
- **Binding constraint:** claim.theta subject to claim_incentive
- **Sensitivity:** claim.mean_bps is steep in claimThresholdBps across 107.5–150 %: it rises as claimThresholdBps increases. Insensitive at ±1 step: ST of factor 'G3 group' (grouped) — system P(bad debt) 0.23, minting halted by price (h/yr) 0.00, false activation halts (h/yr) 0.00, oracle attack share 0.00.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (10,750 bps (107.5 %)): violates claim_incentive; +1 step (11,250 bps (112.5 %)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

**claimThresholdBps** — the collateral ratio at pClaim below which a matured vault is claimable (RED-4(a)) and the claimant's payout margin (RED-5) (rules RED-4, RED-5). Provisionally keep 11000. Decision rule: claimThresholdBps = the smallest threshold (250-bps lattice) at which the expected claimant margin at the first RED-4(a) trigger — collateral less FEE-1 and the network fee, sold at the true price less the policy slippage, minus the YED burned — is ≥ claimant_min_profit_bps (worst ensemble member); KEEP unless more than materiality lower; BLOCKED (highest margin) when no threshold within bounds clears it. Binding: claim.theta subject to claim_incentive. At current: mean claimant margin (bps) 223; show-up rate 61.9%; already bad at trigger 22.3%; owner forfeit (bps) 511. claim.mean_bps is steep in claimThresholdBps across 107.5–150 %: it rises as claimThresholdBps increases. Design note G3-DN2: RED-5 residual is always 0 under RED-4(a) — The claimant cap uses the same threshold that made the vault underwater, so a RED-4(a) claimant takes the whole collateral; an absent owner forfeits everything above the debt (mean forfeit at the first trigger: 511 bps of the debt). Design note G3-DN3: pClaim lag eats the claim margin — pClaim = max(pMid, pSlow) lags a falling price, so when a vault first shows underwater the claimant's expected margin is 223 bps of the debt at the current threshold (show-up rate 62%; already bad debt at the trigger: 22%). Evidence: synthetic price ensemble only, so the verdict is PROVISIONAL until real YEC data is supplied; confidence low. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

**classMax[2]** — the longest lock (blocks) of a term class (MINT-2) (rules MINT-2). Provisionally keep 2102400. Decision rule: Outer class ends (shortest and longest lock) are product choices: verified, not tuned — KEEP while MINT-2 contiguity and the CLTV height bound hold. Binding: verification: every constraint holds (locktime). At current: heterogeneity A 2.58; C 0.74. classMax[2] is verified, not swept for a metric. Design note G3-DN1: No liquidation before lockHeight + grace (fact 1.5-1) — The vault script admits only the owner path until claimHeight, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown. P(bad debt) at the shipped ratios: A 1.38%, B 7.25%, C 32.04% (tolerances 0.50%, 1.00%, 2.00%); the lowest reachable within the registry bounds: A 0.32%, B 2.74%, C 20.43%. At the upper bound each class meets its tolerance only for terms up to A 71 d, B 0 d, C 0 d (class maxima 90, 365, 1825 d), and a vault that is bad at the claim opening is short by 19%, 41%, 57% of its debt on average. A bad vault is not yet a realised loss: nobody may claim it below the debt, it stays claimable if the price recovers, and YED holders bear the gap only through the peg (D-RD-AUD-3). Design note G3-DN6: Term heterogeneity inside the classes — P(bad debt) rises with the term inside each class (heterogeneity A 2.58, B 1.19, C 0.74 against 0.5); with three fixed classes (NUM_CLASSES) and one ratio per class, long terms are under-collateralised relative to short ones. Evidence: synthetic price ensemble only, so the verdict is PROVISIONAL until real YEC data is supplied; confidence low. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

Trade-off (ratio → aggregate P(bad debt)): 275% → 33.6%, 325% → 30.7%, 375% → 28.3%, 425% → 26.1%, 475% → 24.1%, 525% → 22.3%, 575% → 21.0%. Least-violating candidate: current. Design note G3-DN1: No liquidation before lockHeight + grace (fact 1.5-1) — The vault script admits only the owner path until claimHeight, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown. P(bad debt) at the shipped ratios: A 0.45%, B 2.74%, C 20.44% (tolerances 0.50%, 1.00%, 2.00%); the lowest reachable within the registry bounds: A 0.32%, B 2.74%, C 20.44%. At the upper bound each class meets its tolerance only for terms up to A 71 d, B 0 d, C 0 d (class maxima 90, 365, 1825 d), and a vault that is bad at the claim opening is short by 19%, 41%, 57% of its debt on average. A bad vault is not yet a realised loss: nobody may claim it below the debt, it stays claimable if the price recovers, and YED holders bear the gap only through the peg (D-RD-AUD-3). Design note G3-DN5: The σ multiplier adds no protection at its median — The median hour-mode σ multiplier over the ensemble is 10000 bps (σ̂ on the smoothed pFast stays below sigmaRefBps, D-WP3-6), so the base ratio alone carries the drawdown budget. Evidence: synthetic price ensemble only, so the verdict is PROVISIONAL until real YEC data is supplied; confidence low. - **Decision rule:** abandonBlocks = the smallest value ≥ max(grace, runbook) — runbook = signalWindow (detect) + signalWindow (W19) + 16,128 (M14) + runbook_operator_buffer_blocks — with P(false abandonment per year) ≤ max_false_abandon_prob at the expected enforcing share (G5 drift mixture), rounded up to whole days; KEEP unless the current value violates the bound or the rule value is more than materiality smaller.

- **Sensitivity:** P(false abandonment) is 0.0e+00/yr at the recommended value and falls geometrically with each extra signal window, so the binding constraint is the runbook / grace lower bound, not false abandonment; every extra day of abandonBlocks is one more day vaults stay unsweepable if the developers are truly gone. Insensitive at ±1 step: ST of factor 'G4 group' (grouped) — system P(bad debt) 0.00, minting halted by price (h/yr) 0.00, false activation halts (h/yr) 0.00, oracle attack share 0.00.

Decision rule: abandonBlocks = the smallest value ≥ max(grace, runbook) — runbook = signalWindow (detect) + signalWindow (W19) + 16,128 (M14) + runbook_operator_buffer_blocks — with P(false abandonment per year) ≤ max_false_abandon_prob at the expected enforcing share (G5 drift mixture), rounded up to whole days; KEEP unless the current value violates the bound or the rule value is more than materiality smaller. Dev-absence variants: abandonment fires before returning developers can finish the freeze-then-fix runbook for 14 d, 30 d, 60 d, 90 d; if they never return, vaults stay unsweepable for 30 days. Design note G4-DN1: Abandonment tolerates only a short developer absence — With abandonBlocks 34560 (30 d), developers who return after more than 12 days cannot ship a freeze-then-fix set (M14 lead 16128 + buffer 4608) before owners may sweep: abandonment and a slow fix race. Evidence: exact math under stated policy assumptions; confidence medium. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-feeMin"></a>§3.37 `feeMin` — KEEP

**50,000,000 zat (0.5 YEC) → 50,000,000 zat (0.5 YEC)** · locked / locked · confidence medium · provenance judgement · *insensitive*
- **What it controls:** Enforcement fee floor (0.5 YEC); 4·feeMin is the MINT-5 floor. Read by: FEE-1, MINT-5, AFEE-1.
- **Decision rule:** feeMin: verify — the MINT-5 floor 4·feeMin fits a minMint class-C vault at worst_price_usd (§1.4), and the owner of a minMint vault (any class, minted at the reference or the worst price) whose collateral has fallen to the claim threshold still gains by redeeming (FEE-1 ≤ 1 − 10⁴/claimThresholdBps of the collateral); else the nearest value that passes.
- **Binding constraint:** verification: every constraint holds at the current value (floor_mintable, edge_redeem)
- **Sensitivity:** fee.edge_fee_share_max is steep in feeMin across 10000000–150000000 zat: it rises as feeMin increases. Insensitive at ±1 step: ST of factor 'G6 group' (grouped) — system P(bad debt) 0.00, minting halted by price (h/yr) 0.00, false activation halts (h/yr) 0.00, oracle attack share 0.00.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (40,000,000 zat (0.4 YEC)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value); +1 step (60,000,000 zat (0.6 YEC)): violates edge_redeem.

#### <a id="p-kSlack"></a>§3.47 `kSlack` — KEEP

**2 → 2** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance judgement · *insensitive*
- **What it controls:** Extra selected attestors beyond mSelect (liveness slack). Read by: selection, BUNDLE-1.
- **Decision rule:** kSlack: the smallest bundle (4 + 74·(m + k) bytes) with P(minting refuses for want of a bundle) ≤ max_attest_unavailability at attestor_uptime and attestor_outage_correlation, also with one seated attestor down, and kSlack ≥ 1 (with no slack a dead selected attestor fails every bundle that picks it, so dormancy never sees it); KEEP unless > materiality.
- **Binding constraint:** primary metric live.bundle_bytes subject to unavail, unavail_outage1, dead_detectable
- **Sensitivity:** live.unavail is steep in kSlack across 0–2: it falls as kSlack increases. Insensitive at ±1 step: ST of factor 'G8 group' (grouped) — system P(bad debt) 0.00, minting halted by price (h/yr) 0.00, false activation halts (h/yr) 0.00, oracle attack share 0.00.

**minMint** — the smallest YED amount one MINT may create (MINT-2) (rules MINT-2). Provisionally keep 10000. Decision rule: minMint ∈ [max(4·feeMin collateral at worst_price_usd, size where the feeMin floor pushes the round-trip fee share above max(max_fee_share_small, the class's proportional share) at the reference price), maxMint]; KEEP inside the range, else the nearest admissible $10 step. Binding: inside [4000, 100000]: verification (4·feeMin at the worst price / maxMint). At current: fee share A 1.45%; B 1.40%; C 1.20%; floor binds above $/YEC 1.20. fee_share.A@worst is steep in minMint across 4000–30000 cents: it falls as minMint increases. Admissible range: [4000, 100000] (lower: 4·feeMin at the worst price; upper: maxMint). FEE-1 is a fixed share of the collateral above the feeMin floor, so the round-trip fee is 1.45% / 1.40% / 1.20% of the debt for A/B/C at every size: minMint cannot bring class A under max_fee_share_small (design note G9-DN1). Design note G9-DN2: feeMin is denominated in YEC — Above $1.20/YEC the 0.5-YEC floor, not feeBps, sets a minMint vault's fee; at the policy's worst price the round-trip share of a minMint debt is 100% (class C). Evidence: synthetic price ensemble only, so the verdict is PROVISIONAL until real YEC data is supplied; confidence low. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

## 5. Design notes

### 1. No liquidation before lockHeight + grace (fact 1.5-1) (G3-DN1)
*G3 · baseRatioBps[0], baseRatioBps[1], baseRatioBps[2], classMax[2]*

- **Finding:** The vault script admits only the owner path until claimHeight, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown. P(bad debt) at the shipped ratios: A 0.45%, B 2.74%, C 20.44% (tolerances 0.50%, 1.00%, 2.00%); the lowest reachable within the registry bounds: A 0.32%, B 2.74%, C 20.44%. At the upper bound each class meets its tolerance only for terms up to A 71 d, B 0 d, C 0 d (class maxima 90, 365, 1825 d), and a vault that is bad at the claim opening is short by 19%, 41%, 57% of its debt on average. A bad vault is not yet a realised loss: nobody may claim it below the debt, it stays claimable if the price recovers, and YED holders bear the gap only through the peg (D-RD-AUD-3).
- **Evidence:** pbad_current: {'A': 0.00453901, 'B': 0.0273813, 'C': 0.204401}; pbad_at_upper_bound: {'A': 0.00316539, 'B': 0.0273813, 'C': 0.204401}; tolerance: {'A': 0.005, 'B': 0.01, 'C': 0.02}; term_reach_days_at_upper_bound: {'A': 71.25, 'B': 0, 'C': 0}; shortfall_at_upper_bound: {'A': 0.000615012, 'B': 0.0113527, 'C': 0.116}
- **Consequence:** Classes whose tolerance cannot be met at any ratio are BLOCKED; capital efficiency of long terms collapses before the risk does.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change (out of scope for tuning): a claim/top-up path during the term (e.g. RED-4 from lockHeight at a higher threshold), term-scaled ratios, or shorter maximum terms.

### 2. RED-5 residual is always 0 under RED-4(a) (G3-DN2)
*G3 · claimThresholdBps, residualMinZat*

- **Finding:** The claimant cap uses the same threshold that made the vault underwater, so a RED-4(a) claimant takes the whole collateral; an absent owner forfeits everything above the debt (mean forfeit at the first trigger: 511 bps of the debt).
- **Evidence:** claim.forfeit_bps: 511.425
- **Consequence:** residualMinZat only matters under RED-4(b); raising claimThresholdBps transfers more value from absent owners to claimants.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: cap the RED-4(a) claimant at debt × (1 + a bounty) and return the rest.

### 3. pClaim lag eats the claim margin (G3-DN3)
*G3 · claimThresholdBps*

- **Finding:** pClaim = max(pMid, pSlow) lags a falling price, so when a vault first shows underwater the claimant's expected margin is 236 bps of the debt at the current threshold (show-up rate 63%; already bad debt at the trigger: 22%).
- **Evidence:** claim.mean_bps: 236.128; claim.show_up: 0.627239; claim.bad: 0.223289
- **Consequence:** Claimants do not show up in a steady decline; abandoned vaults drift into bad debt.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Parameter (claimThresholdBps, see its recommendation) or a rule change: a fresher claim price (e.g. pFast or the attested aClaim for RED-4(a)).

### 4. RED-4(b) never pays a claimant buying YED at par (G3-DN4)
*G3 · emergencyRatioBps*

- **Finding:** Under RED-4(b) the claimant receives the debt's worth at pClaim, the higher of the two prices, so at par it is a loss (2197 bps of the debt on average in the crash runs); it only works as a par exit for YED holders when YED trades at a discount.
- **Evidence:** emerg.b_par_loss_bps: 2196.79; emerg.benefit: -0.0052081; emerg.b_share: 0.5625
- **Consequence:** The emergency path's benefit depends on a YED discount appearing in a crash.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: pay the RED-4(b) claimant at pEmerg (or with a bounty).

### 5. The σ multiplier adds no protection at its median (G3-DN5)
*G3 · baseRatioBps[0], baseRatioBps[1], baseRatioBps[2]*

- **Finding:** The median hour-mode σ multiplier over the ensemble is 10000 bps (σ̂ on the smoothed pFast stays below sigmaRefBps, D-WP3-6), so the base ratio alone carries the drawdown budget.
- **Evidence:** sigma_median_bps: 10000
- **Consequence:** Ratios are sized at multiplier 1×; G2 (sigmaRefBps) decides whether that changes.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Parameter coupling with G2, not a rule change.

### 6. Term heterogeneity inside the classes (G3-DN6)
*G3 · classMin[0], classMin[1], classMin[2], classMax[0], classMax[1], classMax[2]*

- **Finding:** P(bad debt) rises with the term inside each class (heterogeneity A 3.34, B 1.59, C 1.08 against 0.5); with three fixed classes (NUM_CLASSES) and one ratio per class, long terms are under-collateralised relative to short ones.
- **Evidence:** heterogeneity: {'A': 3.33712, 'B': 1.59161, 'C': 1.07925}; max: 0.5
- **Consequence:** Boundary moves only shift the problem between classes.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: more classes, or a ratio that grows with the lock length.

### 7. Abandonment tolerates only a short developer absence (G4-DN1)
*G4 · abandonBlocks*

- **Finding:** With abandonBlocks 34560 (30 d), developers who return after more than 12 days cannot ship a freeze-then-fix set (M14 lead 16128 + buffer 4608) before owners may sweep: abandonment and a slow fix race.
- **Evidence:** abandonBlocks: 34560; max_absence_days: 12
- **Consequence:** A longer abandonBlocks protects slow developers but keeps vaults locked longer when they are truly gone; the parameter cannot serve both.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Policy (set dev_absence_tolerance_days) or a rule change (an explicit developer liveness signal).

### 8. Grace is drawdown exposure for short terms (G4-DN2)
*G4 · grace*

- **Finding:** The grace window adds 0.33% (A), 0.55% (B), 0.80% (C) to P(bad debt) at the shipped values because nobody can claim during it (fact 1.5-1); for class A a 30-day grace is 33–100 % of the term.
- **Evidence:** dP.A: 0.0032994; dP.B: 0.00549526; dP.C: 0.00801543; owner.p_miss: 0.00426666
- **Consequence:** Owner continuity is bought with collateral (G3 must size ratios for lock + grace).
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: allow RED-4 claims from lockHeight at a lower threshold while the owner path stays open.

9. G7 design note (cap-exempt liquidation): the worst-day liquidation demand of class-A vaults alone ($4,278, p95 of the worst crash) exceeds max_depth_fraction of the p10 daily YEC volume ($2,500). Class A at σ = 1 sits exactly at recapRatioBps and bypasses MINT-6 (W20), so supplyCapBps cannot bound it; only a rule change (cap-exempt supply counted against its own limit) or a higher class-A ratio would. The depth figure is a placeholder unless a depth file is loaded. *(G7)*
10. G6 design note (FEE-1 base): FEE-1 is a share of the vault's collateral, so a vault's round-trip fee as a share of its debt is 2·feeBps·baseRatio at every size — 1.78% for class A (ARMED) at the current values, above max_fee_share_small for any feeBps > 11 bps. Levying FEE-1 on the debt value (mintedCents at pMint) would make the fee class-neutral; that is a rule change, out of scope for tuning. *(G6)*
11. an entity with 25% of the seated weight holds 34.8% of a bundle's weight when selected, so it spans qLow in 0% of bundles at the current value (proposal §7.2 states the rule for the *selected* attestors). Griefing only (aMint down → over-collateralisation); theft (aMint up) needs > 65%: P = 0.000. *(G8: qLowBps, qHighBps)*
### 12. feeMin is denominated in YEC (G9-DN2)
*G9 · minMint*

- **Finding:** Above $1.20/YEC the 0.5-YEC floor, not feeBps, sets a minMint vault's fee; at the policy's worst price the round-trip share of a minMint debt is 100% (class C).
- **Evidence:** fee.floor_binds_above_usd: 1.2; fee_share.C@worst: 1
- **Consequence:** minMint (cents) and feeMin (zat) drift apart as the YEC price moves; a USD-stable floor needs a new parameter set after large price moves.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change (feeMin in cents at pMint) or periodic re-sets.
